But I've definitely lost a lot of weight recently, and our department doctor told me I'm way below where I should be.\n\nDoctor: Even though your doctor says you are way underweight, when you look in the mirror, do you still feel that you are too heavy or see parts of your body as carrying too much weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I see myself as \"fat\" in the normal way, but when I look in the mirror, I just obsess over any spot that isn't completely cut or lean. To me, if I'm not the leanest guy on my crew, I feel like I'm failing at being the strong, dependable firefighter I'm supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Since you have lost this weight, have you noticed any changes in your sex drive or sexual function?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I haven't really noticed or thought about that. I've been so exhausted from work and keeping up with my strict routine that it's the last thing on my mind.\n\nDoctor: To help me understand the extent of your weight loss, could you tell me how many pounds you have lost, and what your typical weight was before you started restricting your food?\n\nPatient: I don't know the exact number of pounds I've lost or what my precise starting weight was before all this. I just know that our department doctor took one look at me and said my weight is significantly too low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an intense fear or dread of gaining weight or becoming fat, even if you knew it wouldn't affect your performance at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I get this tight feeling in my chest just thinking about gaining weight or eating high-calorie foods. Even if it didn't affect my performance, I'd still be terrified of looking like I've let myself go. If I'm not completely cut and lean, I just feel like an absolute failure.\n\nDoctor: Since your weight has dropped, have you noticed a complete lack of sexual desire or any physical changes like a loss of morning erections?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about that, to be honest. I've been so drained and lightheaded lately that I haven't paid any attention to things like that. My mind is just constantly focused on my eating routine and keeping my weight down.\n\nDoctor: Even if you don't know the exact numbers, could you give me a rough estimate of your height and what you think you might weigh right now?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know my exact height or weight off the top of my head. I just know that the department doctor told me my weight is significantly lower than it should be, and that it's a major concern for them.\n\nDoctor: Compared to before you started losing weight, would you say your sexual desire has completely disappeared, or have you noticed a significant change in your ability to get or maintain an erection?\n\nPatient: I really haven't thought about that or noticed any changes, to be honest. I'm just so exhausted from work and constantly focused on my eating routine that those things haven't even crossed my mind."
  }
]

I'm a firefighter, and what started as trying to stay lean and fast for work has just gone too far.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced times where you feel you've lost control and eaten a very large amount of food in a short period, or has your eating been strictly limited to restricting your intake?\n\nPatient: No, I've never lost control or overeaten; it's actually the exact opposite. I am extremely strict about restricting my intake, usually just having a protein shake and some grilled chicken to keep my food as minimal as possible. I'm just too terrified that eating any more will add weight and slow me down when I'm out on a call.\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me your current height and weight, and whether you've recently lost a significant amount of weight?\n\nPatient: I don't know my exact height and weight off the top of my head right now. But I've definitely lost a lot of weight recently, and our department doctor told me I'm way below where I should be.\n\nDoctor: Even though your doctor says you are way underweight, when you look in the mirror, do you still feel that you are too heavy or see parts of your body as carrying too much weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I see myself as \"fat\" in the normal way, but when I look in the mirror, I just obsess over any spot that isn't completely cut or lean. To me, if I'm not the leanest guy on my crew, I feel like I'm failing at being the strong, dependable firefighter I'm supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Since you have lost this weight, have you noticed any changes in your sex drive or sexual function?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I haven't really noticed or thought about that. I've been so exhausted from work and keeping up with my strict routine that it's the last thing on my mind.\n\nDoctor: To help me understand the extent of your weight loss, could you tell me how many pounds you have lost, and what your typical weight was before you started restricting your food?\n\nPatient: I don't know the exact number of pounds I've lost or what my precise starting weight was before all this. I just know that our department doctor took one look at me and said my weight is significantly too low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an intense fear or dread of gaining weight or becoming fat, even if you knew it wouldn't affect your performance at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I get this tight feeling in my chest just thinking about gaining weight or eating high-calorie foods. Even if it didn't affect my performance, I'd still be terrified of looking like I've let myself go. If I'm not completely cut and lean, I just feel like an absolute failure.\n\nDoctor: Since your weight has dropped, have you noticed a complete lack of sexual desire or any physical changes like a loss of morning erections?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about that, to be honest. I've been so drained and lightheaded lately that I haven't paid any attention to things like that. My mind is just constantly focused on my eating routine and keeping my weight down.\n\nDoctor: Even if you don't know the exact numbers, could you give me a rough estimate of your height and what you think you might weigh right now?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know my exact height or weight off the top of my head. I just know that the department doctor told me my weight is significantly lower than it should be, and that it's a major concern for them.\n\nDoctor: Compared to before you started losing weight, would you say your sexual desire has completely disappeared, or have you noticed a significant change in your ability to get or maintain an erection?\n\nPatient: I really haven't thought about that or noticed any changes, to be honest. I'm just so exhausted from work and constantly focused on my eating routine that those things haven't even crossed my mind.\n\nDoctor: Did your department doctor mention any other specific health concerns during your checkup, such as an unusually slow heart rate or very low blood pressure?\n\nPatient: I don't recall the department doctor mentioning a slow heart rate or low blood pressure specifically. He was mostly concerned about my significantly low weight and the fact that I nearly fainted during a training drill, which is what triggered this whole evaluation.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel cold much more easily than you used to, or do you find yourself needing to wear extra layers even when it's warm?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed feeling colder than usual or needing to wear extra layers. Mostly, I just feel really tired and drained all the time instead.\n\nDoctor: Did the department doctor run any blood tests, and if so, did they mention if your testosterone levels were unusually low?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'm not really sure if they ran any blood tests, and I don't recall anyone mentioning my testosterone levels. The department doctor was mostly just focused on my low weight and the fact that I nearly fainted during the drill.

Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol). Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation. Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.
